cup rim-hook geometry, and the rotation shifts the wrist or EEF away from the visually selected target\\n- [Find a container's true floor by descend-until-OSC-stall and read the stall height](global/probe-container-floor-by-stall-height.md) \\u2014 you must place a held object on the floor of a container (basket/drawer/box) whose interior floor you cannot see from above (occluded by rim/liner/other objects) and depth/segment centroids are rim-biased\\n- [Anchor duplicate-object wrist refinement to a visible relation landmark.](global/relation-anchored-wrist-refine.md) \\u2014 A task chooses one of multiple identical objects by a spatial relation, and wrist views can contain the wrong duplicate.\\n- [Select identical targets by the named spatial relation before wrist refinement](global/relation-selected-identical-object.md) \\u2014 A task contains duplicate or visually identical objects and names a relation such as on, left of, right of, between, or next to\\n- [Remeasure a held payload after every loaded servo before descending](global/remeasure-loaded-offset-before-descent.md) \\u2014 a large asymmetric object is carried by a handle and precise support placement depends on body-to-EEF offset\\n- [After releasing into a lined basket, retreat straight up before moving laterally](global/retreat-high-before-lateral-near-lined-basket.md) \\u2014 placing items into a woven/cloth-lined basket or any container with a soft rim/liner that stands above the rigid rim; especially multi-item drop-ins where you must traverse away between placements\\n- [Clear a released wrist by reversing the corridor that successfully entered.](global/reverse-entry-corridor-clearance.md) \\u2014 A released object is cavity-side but axial or vertical wrist retreat re-hooks or extracts it.\\n- [Test perimeter contact zones before rejecting a fixture-relative placement.](global/right-front-perimeter-contact.md) \\u2014 A fixture-relative goal says front or side and central visual bands fail despite clean object placement\\n- [Use a capped contact skill to seat rim-perched objects into containers.](global/rim-perch-contact-seat.md) \\u2014 An object has been released at a rimmed container and is visibly perched on the lip or liner while the In predicate is still false.\\n- [A large rotate_wrist yaw drifts the eef ~0.1-0.15m; re-center before descending](global/rotate-wrist-90deg-drifts-eef-recenter-after.md) \\u2014 you rotate_wrist by a large yaw (e.g. 90deg) to align fingers, then need a precise xy over a target\\n- [Disambiguate look-alike cans by RGB label, never by SAM3 brand-noun prompt](global/sam3-brand-noun-can-collision.md) \\u2014 two or more similar cans/cylinders are present and the task names one by brand (\\\"tomato sauce\\\", \\\"alphabet soup\\\", a soup can)\\n- [Re-issue a pick with a nearby landmark when generic picking makes only contact.](global/spatial-qualified-repick.md) \\u2014 A generic Pi0 pick contacts the target object but leaves it upright or ungrasped, and a distinctive nearby landmark is visible in the scene.\\n- [Support an elongated held object on the destination floor before chaining yaw and pitch rotations](global/support-assisted-axis-reorientation.md) \\u2014 an elongated held object must be reoriented inside a shallow container and airborne wrist rotations slip, drift, or exceed the reachable workspace\\n- [Translate a weak hooked grasp with surface support before lifting over thresholds.](global/support-guided-weak-hook-translation.md) \\u2014 A narrow rim or handle hook survives gentle motion but slips under airborne rotation or long carry.\\n- [Re-check duplicate identity before tuning a non-firing placement.](global/verify-duplicate-semantics.md) \\u2014 A task has duplicate objects and repeated visually plausible placements do not trigger the predicate\\n- [Trust visual grasp evidence over the Pi0 pick success flag.](global/visual-over-pick-heuristic.md) \\u2014 A pi0_pick reports success false but the object is visibly moved or held and gripper/image evidence contradicts the heuristic\\n\\n##   "content": "---\nid: libero-in-predicate-fires-while-grasped\nscope: global\nkind: strategy\ntitle: Test placement targets by holding the object over them and watching libero_terminated,\n  before releasing\napplies_when: an In()/On() place task where the correct destination among several\n  look-alikes is uncertain, or where releasing risks tipping/losing the object\nsymptom:\n- wrong compartment\n- which container\n- ambiguous target\n- place predicate not firing\n- cannot re-grasp\n- tipped after release\n- in predicate\nevidence:\n  cells:\n  - 10_task_t5_s0\n  attempts: 4\n  solved_seeds:\n  - 0\nconfidence: single-shot\nrelated:\n- caddy-back-is-near-robot-pocket\n---\n\n\nLIBERO In()/On() success predicates check the OBJECT'S POSE, not the gripper\nstate, so `libero_terminated` flips the moment the held object's pose satisfies\nthe region \u2014 you do NOT have to release first.\n\n**Why:** libero_terminated mirrors the BDDL goal predicate, which is a function\nof object poses only. Releasing is irrelevant to the check (confirmed: placing a\nmug in the correct caddy pocket fired while still grasped; placing/releasing in\nthree WRONG pockets never fired).\n**How to apply:**\n- To disambiguate among candidate destinations, carry the still-grasped object\n  (gripper +1) low over each candidate and read `libero_terminated` from the\n  returned state. Move to the next candidate if it stays false \u2014 no release, no\n  re-grasp, no commitment. This turns an N-way target guess into N cheap probes.\n- Only `release` after the flag is already true (or to seat a confirmed target).\n- Corollary: a non-firing predicate with a clean, low, upright placement is\n  evidence of WRONG DESTINATION, not wrong physics \u2014 switch targets first.\n- Caveat: some tasks pair In() with a \"not in gripper\"/stack-stability term; if a\n  held probe won't fire but the geometry looks perfect, release once to check.\n**Falsify:** a place task where the predicate provably requires the gripper open\n(object released) and a correctly-posed held object does NOT fire.\n**Related:** [[caddy-back-is-near-robot-pocket]]\n"

With enough chunks it continues its learned trajectory,\nwhich tends toward a +y place pose. `success=false` here just meant the\nlift+close heuristic didn't latch (a can's diameter keeps opening >0.06), NOT\nthat the grasp failed.\n\n**How to apply:** Judge the grasp from perception (wrist cam + gripper opening\nconsistent with the object's width), not from the success flag. If the trained\ndrift happens to carry the object over your true target (e.g. a basket at\nrobot-left), let it finish and just settle/release there. If it drifts AWAY from\nthe target, cap max_chunks (<=16, even <=8 for fragile items) so Pi0 stops near\nlift, then script the carry yourself. Never assume the drop location is safe \u2014\ncheck where it went.\n\n**Falsify:** If pi0_pick consistently halts at lift with no lateral drift across\nscenes, this no longer applies.\n\n**Related:** [[sam3-brand-noun-can-collision]]\n"

With enough chunks it continues its learned trajectory,\\nwhich tends toward a +y place pose. `success=false` here just meant the\\nlift+close heuristic didn't latch (a can's diameter keeps opening >0.06), NOT\\nthat the grasp failed.\\n\\n**How to apply:** Judge the grasp from perception (wrist cam + gripper opening\\nconsistent with the object's width), not from the success flag. If the trained\\ndrift happens to carry the object over your true target (e.g. a basket at\\nrobot-left), let it finish and just settle/release there. If it drifts AWAY from\\nthe target, cap max_chunks (<=16, even <=8 for fragile items) so Pi0 stops near\\nlift, then script the carry yourself. Never assume the drop location is safe \\u2014\\ncheck where it went.\\n\\n**Falsify:** If pi0_pick consistently halts at lift with no lateral drift across\\nscenes, this no longer applies.\\n\\n**Related:** [[sam3-brand-noun-can-collision]]\\n\"\n}"

1. Descend to z=1.00 at the current xy (0.058, 0.248) — the bowl bottom would be ~0.96.
2. Check terminated.
If not terminated, probe small xy shifts.

Actually better: first go down to just above the plate (bowl rim at the plate height) then probe xy. But careless descent might crash into the plate — the plate is only ~0.01 thick, so descending to z 0.96 is safe (bowl bottom ~0.93 vs plate top 0.92).

Hmm, what do I know about the held bowl vertical relationship? At step 0 with the bowl on the table (rim z=0.9258, table 0.90). At step 4, after lifting, the eef z = 1.0118 and the bowl rim points measured z ≈ 0.99-1.01 → so the rim is at about the eef z. So rim z ≈ eef z, roughly. So for the bowl rim to be at plate top + plate thickness... the bowl's bottom is ~0.02 below the rim (bowl height 0.026). To have the bowl rest on the plate (top 0.909), bowl bottom should be ≈ 0.91 → rim ≈ 0.93 → eef z ≈ 0.93.

But wait — plate top z: the plate samples gave z ≈ 0.907-0.914 (region median 0.9087). Table z 0.90. The plate is thin (~0.01 thick). So the bowl's bottom should be at ~0.915, rim at eef ≈ 0.935.

However, the bowl is held in a pinch at the rim, so the bowl hangs from its rim which is at the finger height minus a bit (fingers grab the rim, so the rim is between the fingertips; the fingertips are ~1-2 cm below the eef). Let's estimate: at step 4, eef z=1.0118 and the bowl rim (top edge of the bowl) measured 0.99-1.01. So rim ≈ eef_z - 0.01.

So descend target eef z ≈ 0.945 to place the bowl bottom at ~0.915 resting on the plate.

1. move_to (0.058, 0.248, 0.99) — descend 10 cm, check terminated.
2. If not, move_to (0.058, 0.248, 0.955) and re-check; use terminated flag as the probe.
3. Then release and retreat.

But the xy accuracy: bowl center offset from eef was (-0.022, -0.046) as measured at step 4. Current eef (0.0582, 0.2483) → bowl ≈ (0.036, 0.202). Plate center (0.050, 0.203). So bowl is 1.4 cm off in x — well within the plate radius (~0.07). Good.

Actually hold on: the step-4 measurement gave the offset (-0.022, -0.046) using the circle fit through 3 points that included a possibly-spurious pixel (-0.1411,0.2856). Let me sanity check: rim points A(-0.2201,0.3225), B(-0.1902,0.3345), D(-0.2098,0.2959). The fitted center (-0.200,0.315) is 0.023 from A, 0.021 from B, 0.021 from D. But the bowl radius is 0.045! The fit gives radius ~0.022, which is about half the expected radius. Hmm, that suggests my three points were not on the rim circle but rather on the bowl's *body* (the pattern region), i.e. interior points. Then the "center" (-0.200,0.315) is still a plausible center of the bowl.

Anyway: the direction of the offset (bowl = eef + (-0.022,-0.046)) is plausible: bowl center is closer to the robot in x? Hmm, the eef x=0.0582 at step 6, and bowl center should then be 0.036. Do I have any evidence for the x offset? At step 0 the bowl center was (-0.207,0.320) and Pi0 descended... the eef at pick end was (-0.183,0.364) — so the eef ended up at (+0.024, +0.044) relative to the bowl center. i.e., bowl = eef + (-0.024, -0.044). So bowl center = eef - (0.023, 0.045).
